# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 105

> ```diff
> +/// instantly after the update without a direct backward reference (which is
> +/// impossible to be represented in safe Rust).
> ```

`design-decisions` (minor): The rationale claims that a backward reference is impossible in safe Rust, but safe alternatives such as `Weak` references or stable run-queue identifiers exist. This overstates the constraint and obscures the actual ownership, coupling, or locking tradeoff behind the deferred update design.

**Fix.** Replace the impossibility claim with the concrete reason a backward reference was rejected, such as avoiding run-queue coupling or additional synchronization, and briefly mention the alternatives considered.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> +/// To handle this problem, we use a `pending_weight` field to store the new weight.
> +/// ...
> +/// with a bit flag `HAS_PENDING`.
> +/// ...
> +/// `compare_exchange_weak` loop
> ```

`no-impl-in-docs` (minor): The `FairAttr` doc comment exposes private fields, flag packing, atomic operations, and load-count details. These are implementation choices rather than behavior that users of `FairAttr` need to depend on.

**Fix.** Keep the `FairAttr` documentation focused on the deferred weight-update behavior and its guarantees. Move the field layout and `compare_exchange_weak` protocol into an internal `//` comment beside `fetch_weight`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 116

> ```diff
> +/// - If the weight does not need to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING == 0`),
> +/// - If the weight needs to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING != 0`), we try to
> +///   store the new weight into the `weight` field with `IS_PENDING` cleared
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): The documentation repeatedly refers to `IS_PENDING`, but the declared flag is `HAS_PENDING`. Readers cannot directly correlate the documented protocol with the implementation.

**Fix.** Replace all three occurrences of `IS_PENDING` with `HAS_PENDING`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> +    fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
> +        ...
> +        self.vruntime
> +            .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> +
> +        (old_weight, new_weight)
> +    }
> ```

`accurate-names` (major): `fetch_weight` sounds like a weight accessor, but it may consume a pending update and rescale `vruntime`. That substantial mutation is invisible at call sites such as `fair_attr.fetch_weight()`.

**Fix.** Rename the method to describe the state transition, such as `apply_pending_weight`, and document or type its `(old_weight, new_weight)` result so callers can see why both values are returned.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 320

> ```diff
> +        // Equals to:
> +        //
> +        // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> +        // self.total_weight -= weight;
> +        self.total_weight -= old_weight;
> ```

`explain-why` (nit): The comment only restates an algebraic expansion and refers to `weight`, which is not present at this call site. It does not explain why subtracting `old_weight` preserves the run-queue invariant.

**Fix.** Replace the expansion with the intent, for example: `fetch_weight` may apply the pending value, but this entity was counted in `total_weight` using `old_weight`, so removal must subtract that value.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> 144:     pub fn update(&self, nice: Nice) {
> 145:         self.pending_weight
> 146:             .store(nice_to_weight(nice), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 147:         self.weight.fetch_or(HAS_PENDING, Ordering::Release);
> 148:     }
> ```

`careful-atomics` (major): `weight` and `pending_weight` are correlated state, but the update is split across two atomics. A real lost-update interleaving is: `update(W1)` stores `W1` and sets `HAS_PENDING`; `fetch_weight()` reads `W1`; `update(W2)` stores `W2` and its `fetch_or(HAS_PENDING)` observes the flag already set; then `fetch_weight()` CASes `weight` to `W1` and clears the flag. The later `W2` remains only in `pending_weight` with no `HAS_PENDING` bit, so future `fetch_weight()` calls return `W1` forever.

**Fix.** Protect `weight`, `pending_weight`, and the `vruntime` rescale with one lock, or encode the pending transition in one atomic state with a generation/sequence so clearing `HAS_PENDING` cannot discard a newer `update()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> 155:     fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
> 156:         let mut weight = self.weight.load(Ordering::Acquire);
> 157:         if weight & HAS_PENDING == 0 {
> ```

`add-regression-tests` (minor): This is an integer-overflow bug fix in the FAIR scheduler, but the commit adds no regression test. The existing scheduler app tests cover basic `sched_*` syscall behavior, not a FAIR weight change after accumulated runtime or repeated concurrent `sched_setattr` updates, so the overflow and accounting paths can regress silently.

**Fix.** Add a regression test under the scheduler tests that exercises a FAIR task changing nice/weight after accumulated runtime, and include the issue or PR reference in the test comment as required by `add-regression-tests`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 183

> ```diff
> 181:         let vruntime = self.vruntime.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> 182:         self.vruntime
> 183:             .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `vruntime * old_weight` is done in `u64`. `vruntime` is time-derived and can grow past `u64::MAX / old_weight` on a long-running task, so a later nice/weight change wraps in release builds and stores a much smaller `vruntime`, reintroducing the overflow this commit is meant to fix.

**Fix.** Use a widened or checked intermediate for the rescale, and handle out-of-range results explicitly, for example by computing in `u128` and saturating or returning an error before casting back to `u64`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> 338:                 let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> 339:                 if old_weight != weight {
> 340:                     self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> 341:                 }
> 342:                 let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Incorrect resource accounting (major): `total_weight` tracks queued FAIR entities only: `enqueue()` adds a queued entity and `pick_next()` subtracts it when it becomes current. In `update_current()`, the current task is not in `total_weight`, so changing a running FAIR task from weight `1024` to `820` with an empty queue executes `0 + 820 - 1024`, which underflows in debug builds and wraps to a huge queued weight in release builds.

**Fix.** Do not adjust `self.total_weight` for the current task's own weight change. Apply queued-entity weight deltas only while the entity is actually represented in `self.entities`; here, use the new `weight` only for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`.

## Security

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> ```diff
>                  let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
>                  if old_weight != weight {
>                      self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
>                  }
>                  let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```
> ```

Denial of service (major): `self.total_weight` does not include the current task: `pick_next()` subtracts the selected entity before it runs, and `time_slice()` adds `cur_weight` separately. A hostile thread can call `sched_setattr` on itself while it is current and lower its fair weight, e.g. from `nice = 0` to `nice = 19`; with no queued fair peers, `self.total_weight == 0`, so `self.total_weight + weight - old_weight` underflows and can panic the kernel or wrap into bogus scheduler accounting.

**Fix.** Do not adjust `self.total_weight` when reweighting the current task in `update_current()`. Keep the new `weight` for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`, but leave the queued-entity total unchanged.

   189	                _ => {}
   190	            }
   191	            ret
   192	        })
   193	    }

    58	pub(super) struct SchedPolicyState {
    59	    kind: AtomicSchedPolicyKind,
    60	    policy: SpinLock<SchedPolicy>,
    61	}
    62	
    63	impl SchedPolicyState {
    64	    pub fn new(policy: SchedPolicy) -> Self {
    65	        Self {
    66	            kind: AtomicSchedPolicyKind::new(policy.kind()),
    67	            policy: SpinLock::new(policy),
    68	        }
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    pub fn kind(&self) -> SchedPolicyKind {
    72	        self.kind.load(Relaxed)
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    pub fn get(&self) -> SchedPolicy {
    76	        *self.policy.disable_irq().lock()
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    pub fn set(&self, mut policy: SchedPolicy, update: impl FnOnce(SchedPolicy)) {
    80	        let mut this = self.policy.disable_irq().lock();
    81	
    82	        // Keep the old base slice factor if the new policy doesn't specify one.
    83	        if let (
    84	            SchedPolicy::RealTime {
    85	                rt_policy:
    86	                    RealTimePolicy::RoundRobin {
    87	                        base_slice_factor: slot,
    88	                    },
    89	                ..
    90	            },
    91	            SchedPolicy::RealTime {
    92	                rt_policy: RealTimePolicy::RoundRobin { base_slice_factor },
    93	                ..
    94	            },
    95	        ) = (*this, &mut policy)
    96	        {
    97	            *base_slice_factor = slot.or(*base_slice_factor);
    98	        }
    99	
   100	        update(policy);
   101	        self.kind.store(policy.kind(), Relaxed);
   102	        *this = policy;
   103	    }
   104	
   105	    pub fn update<T>(&self, update: impl FnOnce(&mut SchedPolicy) -> T) -> T {
   106	        update(&mut self.policy.disable_irq().lock())
   107	    }
   108	}

impl PerCpuClassRqSet {
    fn pick_next_entity(&mut self) -> Option<SchedEntity> {
        (self.stop.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.real_time.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.fair.pick_next())
            .or_else(|| self.idle.pick_next())
            .and_then(|task| {
                let thread = task.as_thread()?.clone();
                Some((task, thread))
            })
    }

    fn enqueue_entity(&mut self, (task, thread): SchedEntity, flags: Option<EnqueueFlags>) {
        match thread.sched_attr().policy_kind() {
            SchedPolicyKind::Stop => self.stop.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => self.real_time.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::Fair => self.fair.enqueue(task, flags),
            SchedPolicyKind::Idle => self.idle.enqueue(task, flags),
        }
    }

    fn nr_queued_and_running(&self) -> (u32, u32) {
        let queued = self.stop.len() + self.real_time.len() + self.fair.len() + self.idle.len();
        let running = usize::from(self.current.is_some());
        (queued as u32, running as u32)
    }
}

impl LocalRunQueue for PerCpuClassRqSet {
    fn current(&self) -> Option<&Arc<Task>> {
        self.current.as_ref().map(|((task, _), _)| task)
    }

    fn pick_next_current(&mut self) -> Option<&Arc<Task>> {
        self.pick_next_entity().and_then(|next| {
            // We guarantee that a task can appear at once in a `PerCpuClassRqSet`. So, the `next` cannot be the same
            // as the current task here.
            if let Some((old, _)) = self.current.replace((next, CurrentRuntime::new())) {
                self.enqueue_entity(old, None);
            }
            self.current.as_ref().map(|((task, _), _)| task)
        })
    }

    fn update_current(&mut self, flags: UpdateFlags) -> bool {
        if let Some(((_, cur), rt)) = &mut self.current {
            rt.update();
            let attr = &cur.sched_attr();

            let (current_expired, lookahead) = match attr.policy_kind() {
                SchedPolicyKind::Stop => (self.stop.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 0),
                SchedPolicyKind::RealTime => (self.real_time.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 1),
                SchedPolicyKind::Fair => (self.fair.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 2),
                SchedPolicyKind::Idle => (self.idle.update_current(rt, attr, flags), 3),
            };

            current_expired
                || (lookahead >= 1 && !self.stop.is_empty())
                || (lookahead >= 2 && !self.real_time.is_empty())
                || (lookahead >= 3 && !self.fair.is_empty())
        } else {
            true
        }
    }

    fn dequeue_current(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<Task>> {
        self.current.take().map(|((cur_task, _), _)| {
            cur_task.schedule_info().cpu.set_to_none();
            cur_task
        })
    }
}

impl SchedulerStats for ClassScheduler {
    fn nr_queued_and_running(&self) -> (u32, u32) {
        self.rqs.iter().fold((0, 0), |(queued, running), rq| {
            let (q, r) = rq.lock().nr_queued_and_running();
        period_single_cpu * u64::from((1 + num_cpus()).ilog2())
    }

    /// The virtual time slice for each thread in the run queue, measured in vruntime clocks.
    fn vtime_slice(&self) -> u64 {
        self.period() / (self.entities.len() + 1) as u64
    }

    /// The time slice for each thread in the run queue, measured in sched clocks.
    fn time_slice(&self, cur_weight: u64) -> u64 {
        self.period() * cur_weight / (self.total_weight + cur_weight)
    }
}

impl SchedClassRq for FairClassRq {
    fn enqueue(&mut self, entity: Arc<Task>, flags: Option<EnqueueFlags>) {
        let fair_attr = &entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr().fair;
        let vruntime = match flags {
            Some(EnqueueFlags::Spawn) => self.min_vruntime + self.vtime_slice(),
            _ => self.min_vruntime,
        };
        let (_old_weight, weight) = fair_attr.fetch_weight();

        let vruntime = fair_attr
            .vruntime
            .fetch_max(vruntime, Ordering::Relaxed)
            .max(vruntime);

        self.total_weight += weight;
        self.entities.push(Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, vruntime)));
    }

    fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.entities.len()
    }

    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.entities.is_empty()
    }

    fn pick_next(&mut self) -> Option<Arc<Task>> {
        let Reverse(FairQueueItem(entity, _)) = self.entities.pop()?;

        let sched_attr = entity.as_thread().unwrap().sched_attr();
        let (old_weight, _weight) = sched_attr.fair.fetch_weight();
        // Equals to:
        //
        // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
        // self.total_weight -= weight;
        self.total_weight -= old_weight;

        Some(entity)
    }

    fn update_current(
        &mut self,
        rt: &CurrentRuntime,
        attr: &SchedAttr,
        flags: UpdateFlags,
    ) -> bool {
        match flags {
            UpdateFlags::Yield => true,
            UpdateFlags::Tick | UpdateFlags::Wait => {
                let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
                if old_weight != weight {
                    self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
                }
                let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
                self.min_vruntime = match self.entities.peek() {
                    Some(Reverse(leftmost)) => vruntime.min(leftmost.key()),
                    None => vruntime,
                };

                rt.period_delta > self.time_slice(weight)
                    || vruntime > self.min_vruntime + self.vtime_slice()
            }

FN_TEST(sched_param)
{
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_getscheduler(-100), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_getscheduler(1234567890), ESRCH);
	TEST_RES(sched_getscheduler(0), _ret == SCHED_OTHER);

	struct sched_param param;

	TEST_ERRNO(sched_getparam(0, NULL), EINVAL);
	TEST_RES(sched_getparam(0, &param),
		 _ret == 0 && param.sched_priority == 0);

	param.sched_priority = 50;
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setscheduler(0, SCHED_FIFO, NULL), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setscheduler(0, 1234567890, &param), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setscheduler(-100, SCHED_FIFO, &param), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setscheduler(1234567890, SCHED_FIFO, &param), ESRCH);
	TEST_RES(sched_setscheduler(0, SCHED_FIFO, &param), _ret == 0);
	sleep(1);

	TEST_RES(sched_getscheduler(0), _ret == SCHED_FIFO);
	TEST_RES(sched_getparam(0, &param),
		 _ret == 0 && param.sched_priority == 50);

	param.sched_priority = 1234567890;
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setparam(0, NULL), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setparam(-100, &param), EINVAL);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setparam(1234567890, &param), ESRCH);
	TEST_ERRNO(sched_setparam(0, &param), EINVAL);
	param.sched_priority = 51;
	TEST_RES(sched_setparam(0, &param), _ret == 0);
	sleep(1);

	TEST_RES(sched_getparam(0, &param),
		 _ret == 0 && param.sched_priority == 51);
}

 Denial of service (major): `self.total_weight` does not include the current task: `pick_next()` subtracts the selected entity before it runs, and `time_slice()` adds `cur_weight` separately. A hostile thread can call `sched_setattr` on itself while it is current and lower its fair weight, e.g. from `nice = 0` to `nice = 19`; with no queued fair peers, `self.total_weight == 0`, so `self.total_weight + weight - old_weight` underflows and can panic the kernel or wrap into bogus scheduler accounting.
 
-**Fix.** Do not adjust `self.total_weight` when reweighting the current task in `update_current()`. Keep the new `weight` for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`, but leave the queued-entity total unchanged.

# Summary

The change correctly recognizes that FAIR weight updates need to rescale scheduler accounting instead of replacing the weight blindly, and it keeps the existing run-queue operations localized to the scheduler class.

The highest-risk issues are in the new deferred weight-update protocol. `weight` and `pending_weight` are correlated but updated as separate atomics, so a concurrent `sched_setattr` can be lost when `fetch_weight()` clears `HAS_PENDING`. The rescale still multiplies `vruntime * old_weight` in `u64`, leaving another overflow path in the integer-overflow fix. The `total_weight` adjustment in `update_current()` also applies a queued-entity delta to the current task, which is not present in `total_weight`; lowering a running task's weight can underflow or corrupt FAIR accounting, with a denial-of-service path through scheduler syscalls.

Structurally, the new mechanism would be easier to audit if the mutating accessor were named for its side effects, the public docs described behavior instead of atomic internals, and the pending flag name in the docs matched the code. Add a focused regression test for the weight-change overflow/accounting path.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 105

> ```diff
> +/// instantly after the update without a direct backward reference (which is
> +/// impossible to be represented in safe Rust).
> ```

`design-decisions` (minor): The rationale claims that a backward reference is impossible in safe Rust, but safe alternatives such as `Weak` references or stable run-queue identifiers exist. This overstates the constraint and obscures the actual ownership, coupling, or locking tradeoff behind the deferred update design.

**Fix.** Replace the impossibility claim with the concrete reason a backward reference was rejected, such as avoiding run-queue coupling or additional synchronization, and briefly mention the alternatives considered.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> +/// To handle this problem, we use a `pending_weight` field to store the new weight.
> +/// ...
> +/// with a bit flag `HAS_PENDING`.
> +/// ...
> +/// `compare_exchange_weak` loop
> ```

`no-impl-in-docs` (minor): The `FairAttr` doc comment exposes private fields, flag packing, atomic operations, and load-count details. These are implementation choices rather than behavior that users of `FairAttr` need to depend on.

**Fix.** Keep the `FairAttr` documentation focused on the deferred weight-update behavior and its guarantees. Move the field layout and `compare_exchange_weak` protocol into an internal `//` comment beside `fetch_weight`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 116

> ```diff
> +/// - If the weight does not need to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING == 0`),
> +/// - If the weight needs to be updated (i.e. `weight & IS_PENDING != 0`), we try to
> +///   store the new weight into the `weight` field with `IS_PENDING` cleared
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): The documentation repeatedly refers to `IS_PENDING`, but the declared flag is `HAS_PENDING`. Readers cannot directly correlate the documented protocol with the implementation.

**Fix.** Replace all three occurrences of `IS_PENDING` with `HAS_PENDING`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> +    fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
> +        ...
> +        self.vruntime
> +            .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> +
> +        (old_weight, new_weight)
> +    }
> ```

`accurate-names` (major): `fetch_weight` sounds like a weight accessor, but it may consume a pending update and rescale `vruntime`. That substantial mutation is invisible at call sites such as `fair_attr.fetch_weight()`.

**Fix.** Rename the method to describe the state transition, such as `apply_pending_weight`, and document or type its `(old_weight, new_weight)` result so callers can see why both values are returned.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 320

> ```diff
> +        // Equals to:
> +        //
> +        // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> +        // self.total_weight -= weight;
> +        self.total_weight -= old_weight;
> ```

`explain-why` (nit): The comment only restates an algebraic expansion and refers to `weight`, which is not present at this call site. It does not explain why subtracting `old_weight` preserves the run-queue invariant.

**Fix.** Replace the expansion with the intent, for example: `fetch_weight` may apply the pending value, but this entity was counted in `total_weight` using `old_weight`, so removal must subtract that value.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> 144:     pub fn update(&self, nice: Nice) {
> 145:         self.pending_weight
> 146:             .store(nice_to_weight(nice), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 147:         self.weight.fetch_or(HAS_PENDING, Ordering::Release);
> 148:     }
> ```

`careful-atomics` (major): `weight` and `pending_weight` are correlated state, but the update is split across two atomics. A real lost-update interleaving is: `update(W1)` stores `W1` and sets `HAS_PENDING`; `fetch_weight()` reads `W1`; `update(W2)` stores `W2` and its `fetch_or(HAS_PENDING)` observes the flag already set; then `fetch_weight()` CASes `weight` to `W1` and clears the flag. The later `W2` remains only in `pending_weight` with no `HAS_PENDING` bit, so future `fetch_weight()` calls return `W1` forever.

**Fix.** Protect `weight`, `pending_weight`, and the `vruntime` rescale with one lock, or encode the pending transition in one atomic state with a generation/sequence so clearing `HAS_PENDING` cannot discard a newer `update()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> 155:     fn fetch_weight(&self) -> (u64, u64) {
> 156:         let mut weight = self.weight.load(Ordering::Acquire);
> 157:         if weight & HAS_PENDING == 0 {
> ```

`add-regression-tests` (minor): This is an integer-overflow bug fix in the FAIR scheduler, but the commit adds no regression test. The existing scheduler app tests cover basic `sched_*` syscall behavior, not a FAIR weight change after accumulated runtime or repeated concurrent `sched_setattr` updates, so the overflow and accounting paths can regress silently.

**Fix.** Add a regression test under the scheduler tests that exercises a FAIR task changing nice/weight after accumulated runtime, and include the issue or PR reference in the test comment as required by `add-regression-tests`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 183

> ```diff
> 181:         let vruntime = self.vruntime.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> 182:         self.vruntime
> 183:             .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `vruntime * old_weight` is done in `u64`. `vruntime` is time-derived and can grow past `u64::MAX / old_weight` on a long-running task, so a later nice/weight change wraps in release builds and stores a much smaller `vruntime`, reintroducing the overflow this commit is meant to fix.

**Fix.** Use a widened or checked intermediate for the rescale, and handle out-of-range results explicitly, for example by computing in `u128` and saturating or returning an error before casting back to `u64`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> 338:                 let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> 339:                 if old_weight != weight {
> 340:                     self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> 341:                 }
> 342:                 let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Incorrect resource accounting (major): `total_weight` tracks queued FAIR entities only: `enqueue()` adds a queued entity and `pick_next()` subtracts it when it becomes current. In `update_current()`, the current task is not in `total_weight`, so changing a running FAIR task from weight `1024` to `820` with an empty queue executes `0 + 820 - 1024`, which underflows in debug builds and wraps to a huge queued weight in release builds.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `total_weight` comments: do not adjust `self.total_weight` for the current task's own weight change. Apply queued-entity weight deltas only while the entity is actually represented in `self.entities`; here, use the new `weight` only for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`.

## Security

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
>                  let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
>                  if old_weight != weight {
>                      self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
>                  }
>                  let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Denial of service (major): `self.total_weight` does not include the current task: `pick_next()` subtracts the selected entity before it runs, and `time_slice()` adds `cur_weight` separately. A hostile thread can call `sched_setattr` on itself while it is current and lower its fair weight, e.g. from `nice = 0` to `nice = 19`; with no queued fair peers, `self.total_weight == 0`, so `self.total_weight + weight - old_weight` underflows and can panic the kernel or wrap into bogus scheduler accounting.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `total_weight` comments: do not adjust `self.total_weight` when reweighting the current task in `update_current()`. Keep the new `weight` for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`, but leave the queued-entity total unchanged.

# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs (persona: development, grounding: careful-atomics, severity: major)
   defect: The change implements weight updates with an ad-hoc lock-free scheme across two independent atomics (`weight` with a `HAS_PENDING` bit and `pending_weight`) plus a `compare_exchange_weak` loop. # Summary

The change correctly recognizes that FAIR weight updates need to rescale scheduler accounting instead of replacing the weight blindly, and it keeps the existing run-queue operations localized to the scheduler class.

The highest-risk issues are in the new deferred weight-update protocol. `weight` and `pending_weight` are correlated but updated as separate atomics, so a concurrent `sched_setattr` can be lost when `fetch_weight()` clears `HAS_PENDING`. The rescale still multiplies `vruntime * old_weight` in `u64`, leaving another overflow path in the integer-overflow fix. The `total_weight` adjustment in `update_current()` also applies a queued-entity delta to the current task, which is not present in `total_weight`; lowering a running task's weight can underflow or corrupt FAIR accounting, with a denial-of-service path through scheduler syscalls.

Structurally, the new mechanism would be easier to audit if the mutating accessor were named for its side effects, the public docs described behavior instead of atomic internals, and the pending flag name in the docs matched the code. Add a focused regression test for the weight-change overflow/accounting path.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 105

> ```diff
> +/// instantly after the update without a direct backward reference (which is
> +/// impossible to be represented in safe Rust).
> ```

`design-decisions` (minor): The rationale claims that a backward reference is impossible in safe Rust, but safe alternatives such as `Weak` references or stable run-queue identifiers exist. This overstates the constraint and obscures the actual ownership, coupling, or locking tradeoff behind the deferred update design.

**Fix.** Replace the impossibility claim with the concrete reason a backward reference was rejected, such as avoiding run-queue coupling or additional synchronization, and briefly mention the alternatives considered.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 108

> ```diff
> +/// To handle this problem, we use a `pending_weight` field to store the new weight.
> +/// ...
> +/// with a bit flag `HAS_PENDING`.
> +/// ...
> +/// `compare_exchange_weak` loop
> ```

`no-impl-in-docs` (minor): The `FairAttr` doc comment exposes private fields, flag packing, atomic operations, and load-count details. These are implementation choices rather than behavior that users of `FairAttr` need to depend on.

**Fix.** Keep the `FairAttr` documentation focused on the deferred weight-update behavior and its guarantees. Move the field layout and `compare_exchange_weak` protocol into an internal `//` comment beside `fetch_weight`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 116

> ```diff
> +/// - If the weight does not need to be updated (i.e. `weight {prompt} IS_PENDING == 0`),
> +/// - If the weight needs to be updated (i.e. `weight {prompt} IS_PENDING != 0`), we try to
> +///   store the new weight into the `weight` field with `IS_PENDING` cleared
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): The documentation repeatedly refers to `IS_PENDING`, but the declared flag is `HAS_PENDING`. Readers cannot directly correlate the documented protocol with the implementation.

**Fix.** Replace all three occurrences of `IS_PENDING` with `HAS_PENDING`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> +    fn fetch_weight({prompt}self) -> (u64, u64) {
> +        ...
> +        self.vruntime
> +            .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> +
> +        (old_weight, new_weight)
> +    }
> ```

`accurate-names` (major): `fetch_weight` sounds like a weight accessor, but it may consume a pending update and rescale `vruntime`. That substantial mutation is invisible at call sites such as `fair_attr.fetch_weight()`.

**Fix.** Rename the method to describe the state transition, such as `apply_pending_weight`, and document or type its `(old_weight, new_weight)` result so callers can see why both values are returned.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 320

> ```diff
> +        // Equals to:
> +        //
> +        // self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> +        // self.total_weight -= weight;
> +        self.total_weight -= old_weight;
> ```

`explain-why` (nit): The comment only restates an algebraic expansion and refers to `weight`, which is not present at this call site. It does not explain why subtracting `old_weight` preserves the run-queue invariant.

**Fix.** Replace the expansion with the intent, for example: `fetch_weight` may apply the pending value, but this entity was counted in `total_weight` using `old_weight`, so removal must subtract that value.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> 144:     pub fn update({prompt}self, nice: Nice) {
> 145:         self.pending_weight
> 146:             .store(nice_to_weight(nice), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 147:         self.weight.fetch_or(HAS_PENDING, Ordering::Release);
> 148:     }
> ```

`careful-atomics` (major): `weight` and `pending_weight` are correlated state, but the update is split across two atomics. A real lost-update interleaving is: `update(W1)` stores `W1` and sets `HAS_PENDING`; `fetch_weight()` reads `W1`; `update(W2)` stores `W2` and its `fetch_or(HAS_PENDING)` observes the flag already set; then `fetch_weight()` CASes `weight` to `W1` and clears the flag. The later `W2` remains only in `pending_weight` with no `HAS_PENDING` bit, so future `fetch_weight()` calls return `W1` forever.

**Fix.** Protect `weight`, `pending_weight`, and the `vruntime` rescale with one lock, or encode the pending transition in one atomic state with a generation/sequence so clearing `HAS_PENDING` cannot discard a newer `update()`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 155

> ```diff
> 155:     fn fetch_weight({prompt}self) -> (u64, u64) {
> 156:         let mut weight = self.weight.load(Ordering::Acquire);
> 157:         if weight {prompt} HAS_PENDING == 0 {
> ```

`add-regression-tests` (minor): This is an integer-overflow bug fix in the FAIR scheduler, but the commit adds no regression test. The existing scheduler app tests cover basic `sched_*` syscall behavior, not a FAIR weight change after accumulated runtime or repeated concurrent `sched_setattr` updates, so the overflow and accounting paths can regress silently.

**Fix.** Add a regression test under the scheduler tests that exercises a FAIR task changing nice/weight after accumulated runtime, and include the issue or PR reference in the test comment as required by `add-regression-tests`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 183

> ```diff
> 181:         let vruntime = self.vruntime.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> 182:         self.vruntime
> 183:             .store(vruntime * old_weight / new_weight, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`checked-arithmetic` (major): `vruntime * old_weight` is done in `u64`. `vruntime` is time-derived and can grow past `u64::MAX / old_weight` on a long-running task, so a later nice/weight change wraps in release builds and stores a much smaller `vruntime`, reintroducing the overflow this commit is meant to fix.

**Fix.** Use a widened or checked intermediate for the rescale, and handle out-of-range results explicitly, for example by computing in `u128` and saturating or returning an error before casting back to `u64`.

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
> 338:                 let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
> 339:                 if old_weight != weight {
> 340:                     self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
> 341:                 }
> 342:                 let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Incorrect resource accounting (major): `total_weight` tracks queued FAIR entities only: `enqueue()` adds a queued entity and `pick_next()` subtracts it when it becomes current. In `update_current()`, the current task is not in `total_weight`, so changing a running FAIR task from weight `1024` to `820` with an empty queue executes `0 + 820 - 1024`, which underflows in debug builds and wraps to a huge queued weight in release builds.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `total_weight` comments: do not adjust `self.total_weight` for the current task's own weight change. Apply queued-entity weight deltas only while the entity is actually represented in `self.entities`; here, use the new `weight` only for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`.

## Security

### `kernel/src/sched/sched_class/fair.rs` line 340

> ```diff
>                  let (old_weight, weight) = attr.fair.fetch_weight();
>                  if old_weight != weight {
>                      self.total_weight = self.total_weight + weight - old_weight;
>                  }
>                  let vruntime = attr.fair.update_vruntime(rt.delta, weight);
> ```

Denial of service (major): `self.total_weight` does not include the current task: `pick_next()` subtracts the selected entity before it runs, and `time_slice()` adds `cur_weight` separately. A hostile thread can call `sched_setattr` on itself while it is current and lower its fair weight, e.g. from `nice = 0` to `nice = 19`; with no queued fair peers, `self.total_weight == 0`, so `self.total_weight + weight - old_weight` underflows and can panic the kernel or wrap into bogus scheduler accounting.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `total_weight` comments: do not adjust `self.total_weight` when reweighting the current task in `update_current()`. Keep the new `weight` for `update_vruntime()` and `time_slice()`, but leave the queued-entity total unchanged.
